The purpose of this Condition and Maintenance of Certification requirement is for Certified Health IT developers to demonstrate interoperability and functionality of their certified health IT in real world settings and scenarios, rather than in a controlled test environment with an ONC-Authorized Testing Lab.\n\n## athenahealth's Compliance\n\nathenahealth is one of the ambulatory ONC Certified Health IT developers with the largest market share, along with Epic, eClinicalWorks, Allscripts, and NextGen. athenahealth has published Real World Testing plans for their products, including athenaPractice, which include measures for testing interoperability features such as CCDA creation and certified API functionality.\n\n## Artificial Intelligence Disclosures\n\nThe Assistant Secretary for Technology Policy and Office of the National Coordinator for Health Information Technology (\"ASTP/ONC\") through its Health Data, Technology, and Interoperability: Certification Program Updates, Algorithm Transparency, and Information Sharing (HTI-1) Final Rule requires certified health information technology (\"health IT\") developers to meet specific obligations regarding the use of artificial intelligence (\"AI\") in their certified technology products by December 31, 2024.\n\nathenahealth reviewed and updated internal AI policies and procedures to ensure alignment with HTI-1 and the National Institute of Standard and Technology (\"NIST\") AI Risk Management Framework.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01BYDC7TJjx2Xv3dntPYSKqq","session_id":"dfabe0df-7a0b-4f07-a4fd-79f9f540796c","uuid":"95325cae-bafd-4a28-9081-0d4db04ebee9"}

Algorithm Transparency, and Information Sharing (HTI-1) Final Rule requires certified health information technology (“health IT”) developers to meet specific obligations regarding the use of artificial intelligence (“AI”) in their certified technology products by December 31, 2024. Specifically, ASTP/ONC regulates Predictive Decision Support Interventions (“PDSI”), which means technology that “supports decision-making based on algorithms or models that derive relationships from training data and then produces an output that results in prediction, classification, recommendation, evaluation, or analysis\" that is “supplied as part of the health IT developer’s Health IT module”.\"\n    uid=1_28 StaticText \" \"\n    uid=1_29 StaticText \"The HTI-1 Rule requires that each PDSI is subject to intervention risk management (“IRM”) practices, which include risk analysis, risk mitigation, and governance. While athenahealth has instituted internal policies and procedures governing the use of certain types of AI for years, athenahealth reviewed and updated internal AI policies and procedures to ensure alignment with HTI-1 and the National Institute of Standard and Technology (“NIST”) \"\n    uid=1_30 link \"AI Risk Management Framework\" url=\"https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/NIST.AI.100-1.pdf\"\n      uid=1_31 StaticText \"AI Risk Management Framework\"\n    uid=1_32 StaticText \". athenahealth designed its IRM practices to align with ASTP/ONC’s FAVES principles – fair, appropriate, valid, effective, and safe. In addition to the FAVES principles, as required by HTI-1, athenahealth’s IRM practices assess a functionality’s reliability, robustness, intelligibility, security, and privacy.\"\n    uid=1_33 StaticText \" \"\n    uid=1_34 StaticText \"PDSI at athenahealth is subject to multistage, cross-functional review and governance. To comply with company standards, athenahealth employees developing functionality that includes PDSI are required to review and comply with all applicable policies and procedures, including completing risk assessments to identify ethical, security, data privacy, equity, operational and regulatory risks in new functionality.  Once risks are identified, strategies to control and mitigate such risks are implemented to adjust or safeguard algorithms, data input, or user interfaces. If critical risks are identified, the proposed PDSI is escalated to a responsible AI council staffed with senior subject matter experts to review and assess the functionality and its necessity.\"\n    uid=1_35 StaticText \" \"\n    uid=1_36 StaticText \"athenahealth’s IRM practices review and address AI model trustworthiness, development, testing, deployment, data handling, and decommissioning through risk-based requirements on performance measurement, AI model monitoring, incident management, and internal audits. Monitoring processes are enforced to detect new or emerging risks post-implementation. Relevant stakeholders track, evaluate and internally report adverse incidents related to PDSI and inform, as needed, PDSI users and relevant stakeholders. All AI models will be subject to review and monitoring to ensure compliance with internal policies and regulatory requirements.\"\n    uid=1_37 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=1_38 StaticText \" \"\n    uid=1_39 StaticText \"ASTP/ONC CERTIFIED HEALTH IT\"\n    uid=1_40 heading \"Product Disclaimers and Additional Costs\" level=\"5\"\n    uid=1_41 link \"athenaClinicals View costs\" url=\"https://www.athenahealth.com/sites/default/files/media_docs/ONC-Cert-Update-Clinicals-Additional-Costs-v25_editionless_04.15.25.pdf\"\n      uid=1_42 StaticText \"athenaClinicals\"\n      uid=1_43 StaticText \"View costs\"\n    uid=1_44 link \"athenaClinicals for Hospitals and Health Systems View costs\" url=\"https://www.athenahealth.com/sites/default/files/media_docs/ONCCert-Update-Clinicals-for-HHS-Additional-Costs-v25_editionless_04.15.25_2.pdf\"\n      uid=1_45 StaticText \"athenaClinicals for Hospitals and Health Systems\"\n      uid=1_46 StaticText \"View costs\"\n    uid=1_47 link \"athenaPractice/ athenaFlow View costs\" url=\"https://www.athenahealth.com/sites/default/files/media_docs/ONC-Cert-Update-Additional-Costs-aP_aF-v24_editionless_04.15.25.pdf\"\n      uid=1_48 StaticText \"athenaPractice/ athenaFlow\"\n      uid=1_49 StaticText \"View costs\"\n    uid=1_50 heading \"Real World Testing\" level=\"2\"\n    uid=1_51 StaticText \"Beginning with calendar year 2022, the Assistant Secretary for Technology Policy/Office of the National Coordination for Health IT (ASTP/ONC) requires that vendors of certified health IT demonstrate that certain features related to the interoperability of systems are proven to function as certified in the “real world.” To do so, vendors must publish a plan and corresponding results on an annual basis. This includes a managed care organization, health insurer, military health plan, HMO, preferred provider organization, third party administrator, Medicare/Medicaid plan, and carrier or intermediary to whom physicians submit third party payment claims. Some of these organizations may be forwarding claims to others for actual adjudication, processing, payment or other important steps in the payment process. Since physicians normally cannot bargain for or dictate the terms of these relationships between payers, and since it may be difficult for physicians to track or monitor movement of claims reliably from one payer to another under these relationships, it is our opinion that the physician’s vantage point is valuable, and we have therefore arranged the data to generally reflect \"payers\" to be those entities where claims are submitted by physicians in the first instance.\"\n    uid=2_127 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_128 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_129 StaticText \"All of the data reflected on this site are derived from the experience of athenahealth and its clients using athenahealth’s claims submission, tracking, and follow-up services. The data represent experience during the data collection period by and on behalf of athenahealth physician clients in transactions with the payers listed and/or in transactions on the claims submitted to the payers listed. The rankings and data presented do not and are not intended to present or ascribe causes for that experience, and \"performance\" is intended to refer to measure of observed results when claims were submitted using our services to a payer.\"\n    uid=2_130 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_131 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_132 StaticText \"As noted above in the definition of payer, the activity of a given payer may vary depending on the role that it assumes in the claim process; and, PayerView data results for a given payer may be caused in whole or in part by the actions or processes of others, including other payers, with whom or for whom the payer in turn conducts business. Similarly, the measures chosen for performance are affected by time lags, mistakes, processing practices and data quality on the part of the physician practices involved, by athenahealth itself and by third parties who act as intermediaries in the transmission of claims information. In some cases, such intermediaries are specified or approved by the payer; and, in other cases they may be chosen by other intermediaries or by athenahealth and may be outside of payer control. We actively work to identify and to address matters that we or our customers can control that affect these and other measures, and we have adopted processes that differ from others in the industry. The performance metrics experienced with the same payers by other physicians using other services may have differed materially for the data collection period.\"\n    uid=2_133 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_134 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_135 StaticText \"The reasons for results on any of the measures reported in this site are likely to be complex, and athenahealth looks forward to working with any payer to identify and eliminate causes of adverse experience in any of the reported categories.\"\n    uid=2_136 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_137 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_138 StaticText \"Performance Metrics and Weighting:\"\n    uid=2_139 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_140 StaticText \"To set overall national and regional rankings, we weighted each measure, placing a priority on fast, complete payment. When claims submitted to a payer were paid quickly and fully, the payer tended to do well in the overall ranking. The weighting reflects our opinion as to the impact each measure has on the ability of athenahealth and its providers to receive payment on claims.\"\n    uid=2_141 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_142 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_143 StaticText \"Days in Accounts Receivable (DAR): \"\n    uid=2_144 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_145 StaticText \"Average length of time it takes to receive payment as measured from the date of charge entry to the date of remittance post.\"\n    uid=2_146 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_147 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_148 StaticText \"PayerView’s DAR measures cash flow by dividing the average dollars outstanding by the average daily charge. This measurement encompasses the period from the day a charge is entered into athenaNet to the day payment for a charge is posted. This includes any lag time that was caused by athenahealth or its clients in the transmission and posting process. This lag time is one of several ways this metric could have been influenced by matters outside the control of the payer. However, we believe that claim submission and posting procedures tend to impact the outcome consistently across payers, making data comparative and therefore instructive.\"\n    uid=2_149 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_150 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_151 StaticText \"First Pass Resolve Rate: \"\n    uid=2_152 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_153 StaticText \"Percent of claims that are successfully resolved on the initial submission (e.g., paid or transferred to patient responsibility).\"\n    uid=2_154 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_155 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_156 StaticText \"“First Pass Resolved” claims are classified as receiving full or partial payment and/or deemed as patient responsibility by the payer on the initial claim submission. Partial payments count as a positive outcome only if the payer communicates that no outstanding amount is remaining. Because athenahealth’s client base is a subset of the national provider base, these figures may differ from the national average.\"\n    uid=2_157 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_158 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_159 StaticText \"Provider Collection Burden: \"\n    uid=2_160 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_161 StaticText \"Percent of charges transferred from the primary insurer to the next responsible party. Includes co-insurance, deductibles and other transfers (e.g., non-covered services). Co-pays and Real Time Adjudication (RTA) amounts are not included in the rate as this information is readily known at the time of service.\"\n    uid=2_162 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_163 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_164 StaticText \"A shift in financial burden is imposed on providers when they become responsible for managing collections from the next responsible party after the time of service. Given that providers have few point-of-service tools to manage this growing segment of their revenue stream, it is important to measure what percentage of liability is only available after a patient’s visit and requires providers to incur additional costs to collect those payments.\"\n    uid=2_165 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_166 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_167 StaticText \"Denial Rate: \"\n    uid=2_168 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_169 StaticText \"Percent of claims (both pended and denied) that require the practice to perform back-end rework.\"\n    uid=2_170 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_171 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_172 StaticText \"The more rework involved with a claim, the longer it takes for the provider to get paid and the greater the resources required by physicians to arrange payment. To measure the frequency of these events, PayerView calculates the number of outright denials for claims submitted to each payer, as well as the line items that require reworking. Because we define denials in this calculation as all claims requiring back-end work, this metric may include claims at the payer pending additional information. Moreover, since back-end work is applied by physicians or by athenahealth, there may be subjective factors that affect this percentage. However, we believe these factors tend to impact the outcome consistently across payers.\"\n    uid=2_173 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_174 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_175 StaticText \"Enrollment Efficiency: \"\n    uid=2_176 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_177 StaticText \"Quantitative ranking of administrative burden surrounding provider enrollment in electronic transactions.\"\n    uid=2_178 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_179 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_180 StaticText \"Ranking considers quantitative scores for enrollment in EDI, ERA, EFT, & PAYTO. Scores assigned based on enrollment type, signature requirements, and submission method.\"\n    uid=2_181 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_182 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_183 StaticText \"ERA Transparency: \"\n    uid=2_184 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_185 StaticText \"Percentage of electronic remittance advice (ANSI 835) denial messages with actionable explanations and clear next steps.\"\n    uid=2_186 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_187 StaticText \"This metric reflects how well the payer has adopted the standard code set by returning clear adjustment reason codes accompanied by remark codes as appropriate. The inclusion of the ERA metric, along with the Eligibility Accuracy metric, brings more visibility to the full HIPAA covered ANSI standard transaction suite.\"\n    uid=2_188 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_189 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_190 StaticText \"Eligibility Accuracy: \"\n    uid=2_191 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_192 StaticText \"Correlation of eligibility response to adjudication outcome. Measures how well the payer’s eligibility transaction predicts the outcome of a claim. Eligibility loss categories are:\"\n    uid=2_193 StaticText \"Patient eligible—claim denial\"\n    uid=2_194 StaticText \"Patient ineligible—no claim denial\"\n    uid=2_195 StaticText \"Patient not found—no claim denial\"\n    uid=2_196 StaticText \"Other payer info received—no coordination of benefits (COB) denial\"\n    uid=2_197 StaticText \"No other payer info received—COB denial\"\n    uid=2_198 StaticText \"Any eligibility transaction attempt when the payer’s eligibility system is not available due to unscheduled downtime.\"\n    uid=2_199 StaticText \"Note that PayerView does not measure the amount of money practices receive from payers; it quantifies, rather, the challenges practices face when collecting from payers for services.\"\n    uid=2_200 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_201 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_202 StaticText \"Eligibility transactions are most effective and useful to providers when they are available (i.e., do not encounter persistent downtime or unverified patient responses) and reliable (i.e., the eligibility response indicates how we can expect the claim to be adjudicated). It is important for payers to return a reliable and detailed eligibility response so that providers can identify and resolve eligibility related issues before submitting the claim to the payer.\"\n    uid=2_203 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_204 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_205 StaticText \"Benefit Reliability: \"\n    uid=2_206 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_207 StaticText \"Percent of patient responsibilities in which payer returned the correct patient responsibility information through eligibility at the time of service.\"\n    uid=2_208 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_209 StaticText \"For copays, this means having an exact match to the eventual copay amount. For deductible or coinsurance, it is only required that the eligibility response indicate that the patient may owe a deductible or coinsurance.\"\n    uid=2_210 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_211 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_212 StaticText \"Enrollment Turnaround Time:\"\n    uid=2_213 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_214 StaticText \"athenahealth has added one new weighted metric to capture payer performance in enrollment turn-around-time, which can be an administrative burden for providers.\"\n    uid=2_215 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_216 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_217 StaticText \"The number of days required for a payer to return an enrollment request. Source: iData Research (2008)\"\n    uid=2_348 StaticText \"Assumptions:\"\n    uid=2_349 StaticText \"In developing this calculator, we relied on our experience with our clients in assuming that a practice will generally have one label printer for every three providers and that a medical records technician will take between one and two minutes to prepare and send an internal document of the practice to athenahealth (documents from third parties are faxed directly to athenahealth without any action required on the part of the practice). We have also assumed that a practice's providers are all eligible ambulatory health care providers and therefore qualify for CMS EHR incentive programs and are subject to Clinical Quality Measure penalties.\"\n    uid=2_350 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_351 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_352 StaticText \"Calculations:\"\n    uid=2_353 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_354 StaticText \"The data and assumptions provided above are used to calculate the results in each of the following categories:\"\n    uid=2_355 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_356 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_357 StaticText \"Increase Collections\"\n    uid=2_358 StaticText \": To determine the estimated increase in monthly collections due to use of athenaCollector®, the Average Monthly Collections for a practice of the selected specialty (a per-provider average) is multiplied by the specified number of practice providers to estimate total practice monthly collections. This number is presented to the user during the initial questions as the default, but it, like the other defaults in the calculator, may be changed by the user. The entered practice monthly collections is then multiplied by the entered percentage increase in collections. 6% is offered as the default for the percentage increase in collections because that is the average increase for our clients, based on a weighted average for athenahealth clients with valid pre-athenahealth benchmark data that had their 15-month anniversary with athenahealth during 2013.\"\n    uid=2_359 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_360 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_361 StaticText \"Reduce No-Shows\"\n    uid=2_362 StaticText \": To determine the number of additional visits each month due to use of athenaCommunicator, the entered number of monthly visits (as described in Increase Patient Volume) is multiplied by the Pre-athena No-show Rate and then the expected percentage improvement in the no-show rate. 8% is offered as the default for the percentage improvement in the no-show rate, because our clients have seen at least that percentage of improvement, based on a comparison of the highest no-show rate among clients with athenaCommunicator® and the average rate for appointments without the service for the year ending 2014.\"\n    uid=2_363 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_364 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_365 StaticText \"The expected number of additional visits each month is then monetized by the same method set forth in Increase Patient Volume.\"\n    uid=2_366 StaticText \"Population Health Campaigns\"\n    uid=2_367 StaticText \":\"\n    uid=2_368 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_369 StaticText \"Total revenue per provider received for corresponding patient visit within 14 days of a successful reminder contact as part of a quality measure campaign (listed below) for the year 2014. \"Qualification for Meaningful Use penalties\"\n    uid=2_475 StaticText \": For 2016, penalty total reflects the -2% reimbursement cut of the practice’s total Medicare Part B payments for the year as determined by CMS based on a provider’s 2015 performance. To determine a provider's expected total Medicare payments for the year, the entered practice monthly collections is divided by the specified number of providers to obtain a per-provider average. This average and the Medicare Part B Percentage for a practice of the selected specialty are then multiplied and annualized. \"\n    uid=2_476 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_477 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_478 StaticText \"Qualification for PQRS penalties\"\n    uid=2_479 StaticText \": Annual penalty for PQRS & VM in 2016 reflects the -4% Medicare Part B reimbursement cut as determined by CMS based on a provider’s 2014 performance (% penalty amount depends on group size per CMS). This 4% penalty is applied to the practice’s annual Medicare Part B payments. To determine a provider's expected total Medicare payments for the year, the entered practice monthly collections is divided by the specified number of providers to obtain a per-provider average. This average and the Medicare Part B Percentage for a practice of the selected specialty are then multiplied and annualized. \"\n    uid=2_480 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_481 StaticText \"Chronic Care Management (CCM) Incentives: CCM values based on CMS data indicating that two out of three Medicare beneficiaries have two or more chronic conditions: Page 2: https://www.cms.gov/Outreach-and-Education/Medicare-Learning-Network-MLN/MLNProducts/Downloads/ChronicCareManagement.pdf . W\"\n    uid=2_482 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_483 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_484 StaticText \"Document Services Cost Savings\"\n    uid=2_485 StaticText \": As a rough estimate of the monthly document services cost savings from use of athenaClinicals, the Average Annual Chart Pull Savings (a per-physician average) is multiplied by the specified number of practice providers and then added to the Storage & Management Cost Savings for a practice of the specified number of providers (since the iData Research data underlying this calculation are based on the number of practice physicians, the specified number of practice providers is used as a proxy for purposes of this calculation). This number is then divided by twelve to obtain a monthly average document services cost savings.\"\n    uid=2_486 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_487 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_488 StaticText \"In an effort to represent the athenaClinicals cost savings fairly, we then make sure to back out an estimate of the document services expenditures incurred in the use of athenaClinicals. To do this, we subtract the following from the monthly average cost savings: (1) the Average Monthly Printer Cost,  multiplied by the specified number of practice providers and then divided by three (i.e., the assumed number of providers per label printer); (2) the Average Monthly Label Cost   (a per-provider average), multiplied by the specified number of practice providers; and (3) the Average Monthly Labor Cost   (a per-provider average), multiplied by the specified number of practice providers.\"\n    uid=2_489 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_490 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_491 StaticText \"As background, the average monthly costs used in this calculation are determined as follows. Average Monthly Printer Cost   is based on our market research of the going price of suitable label printers, and the average cost of such printers is then divided by twelve to obtain the monthly expenditure (note that this is only an expense in the first year). Average Monthly Label Cost   is based on the average number of bar-code-labeled clinical documents that athenahealth receives from each athenaClinicals client in a month, multiplied by the average cost of a single label (as determined through our market research). Average Monthly Labor Cost is based on our understanding of the time taken to label a practice's internal clinical document (converted to an hourly fraction), multiplied by the average number of such documents that we receive per month from each athenaClinicals client, and then multiplied by the 2009 median U.S. hourly wage for medical record technicians (i.e., $15.04).\"\n    uid=2_492 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_493 StaticText \"*Survey of more than 2,800 Epocrates users to learn more about the shifting medical practice landscape, February 2015.\"\n    uid=2_494 StaticText \"Full Value Calculator:\"\n    uid=2_495 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_496 StaticText \"Disclaimer:\"\n    uid=2_497 StaticText \"Use of the Full Value Calculator is subject to the following conditions and limitations.\"\n    uid=2_498 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_499 LineBreak \"\n\"\n    uid=2_500 StaticText \"The information provided by the Full Value Calculator is based on historical data from a limited set of medical practices, industry-set penalty and incentive qualifications, and user responses to a few select questions. Let me compile the full research report.\n\n---\n\n# athenahealth Product Ecosystem Research Report\n\n## 1. athenaOne Platform -- Full Module Overview\n\n**athenaOne** is athenahealth's fully integrated, cloud-based SaaS platform. It bundles three core modules plus several ancillary capabilities:\n\n**Core Modules:**\n- **athenaClinicals** -- Electronic Health Record (EHR/EMR)\n- **athenaCollector** -- Revenue Cycle Management / Practice Management (billing)\n- **athenaCommunicator** -- Patient Engagement\n\n**Additional Capabilities within athenaOne:**\n- **athenaTelehealth** -- Embedded HIPAA-compliant video visits (3,000+ active customers)\n- **Epocrates** -- Clinical decision support / drug reference (acquired 2013 for $293M)\n- **Marketplace** -- 500+ third-party integrations across 50 digital health capabilities and 60 specialties\n- **Insights Dashboards** -- Analytics/reporting\n- **AI-native features** -- Ambient-sourced diagnosis, clinically inferred diagnoses, Chart Assist (GenAI), AI-powered coding support\n\nThe Fall 2025 release included 185 features/enhancements across all modules.\n\nSources:\n- [athenaOne Solutions Page](https://www.athenahealth.com/solutions/athenaone)\n- [athenaOne Fall 2025 Updates](https://www.athenahealth.com/resources/blog/athenaone-fall-2025-update)\n- [athenaOne Summer 2025 Updates](https://www.athenahealth.com/resources/blog/athenaone-summer-2025-update)\n\n---\n\n## 2. athenaClinicals -- Clinical EHR Module\n\nathenaClinicals was launched in 2005 and handles:\n\n- **Clinical documentation** -- SOAP notes, encounter documentation, specialty-specific templates\n- **Electronic prescribing** (e-prescribing including EPCS for controlled substances)\n- **Lab and diagnostic test ordering** -- computerized provider order entry (CPOE)\n- **Results management** -- lab results, imaging results\n- **Digital health records** -- patient history, past medical history, surgical history, family history, OB history\n- **Medication management** -- active/completed medication lists, fill history\n- **Allergy tracking**\n- **Immunization records**\n- **Clinical task management**\n- **Referrals management**\n- **Document management**\n- **Voice recognition / integrated dictation**\n- **Quality program support** (MIPS, etc.)\n- **Telehealth** (embedded virtual visits)\n- **Clinical decision support** (via Epocrates integration)\n\n**AI Features (2025):**\n- Ambient-sourced suggested diagnosis (captures visit discussion, surfaces diagnoses)\n- Clinically inferred diagnoses (AI predicts and surfaces potential diagnoses with evidence links)\n- Chart Assist (GenAI assistant for chart review, question answering, summarization)\n\nSources:\n- [athenaClinicals AI Features - HIT Consultant](https://hitconsultant.net/2025/09/09/athenahealth-unveils-ai-native-athenaclinicals-with-new-ehr-features/)\n- [athenahealth EHR Features](https://www.ehrinpractice.com/athenahealth-ehr-80.html)\n- [athenaClinicals EMR - FindEMR](https://www.findemr.com/athenaclinicals-emr-software)\n\n---\n\n## 3. athenaClinicals for Hospitals -- Inpatient vs. Ambulatory\n\n**Key finding: The hospital product is NOT the same codebase as ambulatory.** It was assembled from multiple acquisitions:\n\n- **RazorInsights** (acquired January 2015) -- Cloud-based EHR for rural, critical access, and community hospitals (50 beds and under, ~1/3 of US hospital market). Came with ancillary lab, pharmacy, and radiology modules.\n- **webOMR from Beth Israel Deaconess Medical Center** (acquired February 2015) -- One of the first hospital-built inpatient/outpatient EHR systems, developed over 30 years. Intended to accelerate entry into 100+ bed hospitals (~48% of US hospitals).\n- **University of Toledo Medical Center co-development** (announced 2016) -- Partnership to build the inpatient product into a \"disruptive alternative to legacy health IT.\"\n\nThe result is **athenaOne for Hospitals & Health Systems**, which integrates these acquired technologies with athenahealth's ambulatory platform. It is certified for:\n- Computerized Provider Order Entry (CPOE) for medications, laboratory, diagnostic imaging\n- Drug-drug / drug-allergy interaction checks\n- Inpatient medication orders\n\nIt targets critical access hospitals, small hospitals, and health systems needing integrated inpatient + ambulatory EHR on a cloud platform. The v25 certification was achieved March 21, 2025.\n\nSources:\n- [athenahealth Hospital IT Strategy - HCI Innovation Group](https://www.hcinnovationgroup.com/finance-revenue-cycle/article/13026930/most-interesting-vendors-2016-athenahealth-accelerating-its-growth-in-hospital-it)\n- [athenahealth-Beth Israel EHR Deal - Healthcare IT News](https://www.healthcareitnews.com/news/athenahealth-beth-israel-forge-unique-ehr-deal)\n- [athenahealth Inpatient EHR - Healthcare IT News](https://www.healthcareitnews.com/news/athenahealth-build-inpatient-ehr-university-toledo-medical-center)\n\n---\n\n## 4. athenaIDX -- Separate Enterprise Billing Product\n\n**athenaIDX is a separate, distinct product from athenaCollector**, targeting large enterprises.\n\n**Heritage:**\n- Originally developed by **IDX Systems Corporation** (Burlington, Vermont), founded in the 1960s/70s\n- IDX was acquired by **GE Healthcare for $1.2 billion in 2006**, becoming part of Centricity\n- When Veritas Capital acquired GE Healthcare's value-based care assets in April 2018, this became **Virence Health**\n- After Veritas acquired athenahealth in February 2019, Virence was merged into athenahealth\n- In July 2020, **Centricity Business was officially rebranded as athenaIDX**\n\n**athenaIDX vs. athenaCollector:**\n\n| | athenaCollector | athenaIDX |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Target** | Small/midsize practices | Large practices, health systems, hospitals, billing services |\n| **Architecture** | Cloud-native, part of athenaOne | On-premise or cloud-based (legacy architecture) |\n| **Integration** | Tightly integrated with athenaClinicals and athenaCommunicator | Standalone enterprise RCM |\n| **Heritage** | athenahealth-native (since 2000) | IDX Systems -> GE Centricity Business -> Virence -> athenaIDX (50+ years) |\n| **KLAS ranking** | Highest in midsize practice management | Highest in large practice management |\n\nathenaIDX features: scheduling, eligibility, prior authorizations, billing, automated workflows, A/R management, alternative payment model support, collection operations.\n\nSources:\n- [Centricity Business renamed athenaIDX - Healthcare IT News](https://www.healthcareitnews.com/news/athenahealths-centricity-business-now-athenaidx)\n- [athenaIDX Datasheet (PDF)](https://www.athenahealth.com/sites/default/files/media_docs/athenaIDX%20Datasheet.pdf)\n- [Benefits of athenaIDX](https://www.athenahealth.com/resources/blog/benefits-of-athenaIDX)\n- [athenaCollector vs athenaIDX - TrustRadius](https://www.trustradius.com/compare-products/athenacollector-vs-athenaidx)\n\n---\n\n## 5. Corporate History and Ownership\n\n| Date | Event |\n|---|---|\n| 1997 | Founded in San Diego by Jonathan Bush and Todd Park |\n| 2000 | Launched athenaCollector (billing/RCM product) |\n| 2005 | Launched athenaClinicals (EHR) |\n| 2007 | IPO on NASDAQ |\n| 2013 | Acquired **Epocrates** for $293M (clinical decision support / mobile drug reference) |\n| Jan 2015 | Acquired **RazorInsights** (cloud EHR for small hospitals) |\n| Feb 2015 | Acquired **webOMR** from Beth Israel Deaconess (inpatient EHR) |\n| May 2018 | Elliott Management (9% stake) pressured company publicly for takeover at $160/share |\n| Jun 2018 | CEO/founder Jonathan Bush forced to resign; Jeff Immelt (former GE CEO) became chairman |\n| Apr 2018 | Veritas Capital acquired GE Healthcare's value-based care assets (became Virence Health) |\n| Nov 2018 | **Veritas Capital + Elliott/Evergreen** agreed to acquire athenahealth for **$5.7B ($135/share)** |\n| Feb 2019 | Acquisition closed; Virence Health merged into athenahealth |\n| Jul 2020 | Centricity Business rebranded as athenaIDX |\n| Nov 2021 | **Bain Capital + Hellman & Friedman** agreed to acquire athenahealth for **$17B** (from Veritas/Evergreen, who retain minority stakes; GIC and ADIA also coinvested) |\n| Q1 2022 | Bain/H&F acquisition closed |\n\nSources:\n- [Veritas Acquisition - athenahealth](https://www.athenahealth.com/press-releases/veritas-capital-completes-acquisition-of-athenahealth)\n- [Bain/H&F Acquisition - athenahealth](https://www.athenahealth.com/press-releases/athenahealth-acquired-by-hellman-and-friedman-and-bain-capital)\n- [athenahealth-Epocrates acquisition - TechCrunch](https://techcrunch.com/2013/01/07/athenahealth-acquires-maker-of-popular-mobile-health-apps-for-doctors-epocrates-for-293m/)\n- [$17B Deal - Fierce Healthcare](https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/tech/two-private-equity-firms-near-17b-deal-to-acquire-athenahealth-wsj-report)\n- [athenahealth Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athenahealth)\n\n---\n\n## 6. Marketplace Integrations\n\nThe **athenahealth Marketplace** offers:\n- **500+ third-party applications** across 50 digital health capabilities and 60 specialties\n- Apps are vetted for integration, security, and impact\n- Direct integration with athenaOne (no custom APIs needed)\n- Two-layer support: athenahealth manages connectivity; partner manages their solution\n- Categories include: patient engagement, billing optimization, clinical workflow, AI/agentic AI tools, clinical data access, price intelligence, etc.\n\nThe **athenahealth Developer Portal** provides:\n- 800+ API endpoints\n- HL7, C-CDA, and FHIR R4 interfaces\n- SMART on FHIR support\n\nSources:\n- [athenahealth Marketplace](https://marketplace.athenahealth.com/)\n- [Marketplace Partners - 800+ Solutions](https://www.athenahealth.com/solutions/marketplace-partners)\n- [Marketplace Program](https://www.athenahealth.com/solutions/marketplace-program)\n\n---\n\n## 7. Patient Portal (athenaCommunicator + athenaPatient App)\n\n**athenaCommunicator** provides:\n- **Patient portal** (web-based)\n- **athenaPatient mobile app** (iOS and Android)\n- Secure HIPAA-compliant messaging with care team\n- View health history and test results\n- Appointment scheduling and management (self-scheduling)\n- Online check-in (complete documentation before arrival)\n- Bill pay / payment management\n- Automated messaging at scale: appointment reminders, billing reminders, follow-up care alerts, lab result notifications\n- Care plan access\n- Telehealth visit initiation from the portal/app\n- Mass messaging / outreach campaigns\n- No-show reduction tools\n\nAdministrative staff get a dedicated dashboard within athenaOne for patient communication. Practices can also outsource engagement tasks to athenahealth specialists.\n\nSources:\n- [Patient Portal FAQ](https://www.athenahealth.com/patient-login/faq)\n- [Patient Engagement Software](https://www.athenahealth.com/solutions/patient-engagement)\n- [athenaPatient on Google Play](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.athenahealth.athenapatient)\n\n---\n\n## 8. Telehealth\n\n**athenaTelehealth** is embedded directly in athenaOne:\n- HIPAA-compliant video visits from any browser/device with camera\n- Up to 4 participants per session\n- Live closed captions and transcripts\n- Two-way messaging during visits\n- Full integration: scheduling through billing happens entirely within athenaOne\n- 3,000+ active customers\n- Patients can join from the athenaPatient app or web portal\n\nSources:\n- [athenaTelehealth](https://www.athenahealth.com/solutions/telehealth-services)\n- [athenaTelehealth Blog](https://www.athenahealth.com/resources/blog/athenatelehealth-telehealth-telemedicine)\n\n---\n\n## 9. Revenue Cycle Management (athenaCollector)\n\n**athenaCollector** (launched 2000) is the RCM/practice management core of athenaOne:\n- Automated claims submission and tracking\n- **30,000+ billing rules** scrub claims before submission (industry-leading clean claim rates)\n- Claims pre-populated from clinical record\n- Denials identification, tracking, and resolution with root-cause analysis\n- Patient collections / statements\n- Authorization management\n- Medical coding support (with AI-assisted coding)\n- Eligibility verification\n- Payment posting\n- Reporting and analytics\n- **160,000+ providers** use the platform\n- Revenue cycle services (outsourced billing support available)\n\nThe business model is notable: athenahealth charges a percentage of client collections rather than licensing fees.\n\nSources:\n- [Practice Management Software](https://www.athenahealth.com/solutions/practice-management)\n- [Revenue Cycle Management](https://www.athenahealth.com/solutions/revenue-cycle-management)\n- [athenaCollector Review - business.com](https://www.business.com/reviews/athenacollector/)\n\n---\n\n## 10. FHIR API and Data Exchange\n\n**API Capabilities:**\n- **800+ API endpoints** (REST)\n- **FHIR R4** implementation based on US Core Implementation Guide (STU3)\n- **SMART on FHIR** support\n- HL7 v2 interfaces\n- C-CDA document exchange\n- TEFCA connectivity (via CommonWell/Ellkay platform migration)\n- Postman collections available for FHIR R4 APIs\n\n**Supported FHIR Resources (from EHI Export documentation):**\n\n*System Resources (6):* Location, Medication, Organization, Practitioner, PractitionerRole, OperationOutcome\n\n*Clinical Resources (19+):* AllergyIntolerance, Binary, CarePlan, CareTeam, ClinicalImpression, Condition, Consent, Device, DiagnosticReport, DocumentReference, Encounter, FamilyMemberHistory, Goal, Immunization, MedicationAdministration, MedicationRequest, MedicationStatement, Observation, Procedure, Provenance, ServiceRequest\n\n*Practice Management Resources (8):* Account, Appointment, Coverage, Patient, RelatedPerson, Schedule, Slot\n\n*Custom/Billing Resources:* Adjustment, BillingStatement, Charge, Claim, Collection, Deductible, Eligibility, PatientInsurance, Payment\n\nSources:\n- [FHIR APIs Documentation](https://docs.athenahealth.com/api/docs/fhir-apis)\n- [Developer Portal Overview](https://docs.athenahealth.com/api/guides/overview)\n- [EHI Export FHIR Resources](https://docs.mydata.athenahealth.com/fhir-r4/ehiexport.html)\n- [Athena Core Implementation Guide](https://fhir.athena.io/athenacoreext/index.html)\n- [R4 API Documentation](https://mydata.athenahealth.com/fhirapidoc/r4)\n\n---\n\n## 11. Specialty-Specific Features\n\nathenaOne serves 60+ specialties with tailored workflows, including:\n- **Cardiology** -- specialty workflows, more accessible patient information\n- **Dermatology** -- image management, procedure documentation, cosmetic treatment records\n- **Pediatrics** -- growth charts, immunization tracking, well-child workflows\n- **Ophthalmology** -- integrated with specialty imaging\n- **Family Medicine, Internal Medicine** -- primary care workflows\n- **Orthopedics, Gastroenterology, Endocrinology, Emergency Medicine** -- specialty templates\n\nThe platform provides specialty-specific templates, configurable workflows, and the Marketplace offers specialty-focused third-party integrations.\n\nSources:\n- [Specialty Practices](https://www.athenahealth.com/who-we-serve/specialty-practices)\n- [Cardiology EHR](https://www.athenahealth.com/who-we-serve/cardiology)\n- [Specialty Solutions Press Release](https://www.athenahealth.com/press-releases/athenahealth-launching-new-suite-of-tailored-specialty-solutions)\n\n---\n\n## 12. Comprehensive Data Stored by athenahealth\n\nBased on the FHIR resource list, API documentation, and product features, here is the full scope of data the system stores:\n\n**Clinical Data:**\n- Patient demographics (Patient, RelatedPerson)\n- Allergies and intolerances (AllergyIntolerance)\n- Active/completed medication lists and orders (MedicationRequest, MedicationStatement, MedicationAdministration)\n- Conditions/problems/diagnoses (Condition)\n- Encounters/visits (Encounter)\n- Clinical notes / documentation (DocumentReference, Binary)\n- Lab orders and results (ServiceRequest, DiagnosticReport, Observation)\n- Vital signs (Observation)\n- Immunizations (Immunization)\n- Procedures (Procedure)\n- Care plans (CarePlan)\n- Care team assignments (CareTeam)\n- Clinical impressions/assessments (ClinicalImpression)\n- Goals (Goal)\n- Family history (FamilyMemberHistory)\n- Consent records (Consent)\n- Devices (Device)\n- Provenance/audit trail (Provenance)\n\n**Scheduling/Administrative:**\n- Appointments (Appointment)\n- Schedules and slots (Schedule, Slot)\n- Provider information (Practitioner, PractitionerRole)\n- Organization/location data (Organization, Location)\n- Insurance coverage (Coverage)\n- Account information (Account)\n\n**Billing/Financial (Custom Resources):**\n- Claims (Claim)\n- Charges (Charge)\n- Payments -- Inpatient vs. Ambulatory\n\n**Key finding: The hospital product is NOT the same codebase as ambulatory.** It was assembled from multiple acquisitions:\n\n- **RazorInsights** (acquired January 2015) -- Cloud-based EHR for rural, critical access, and community hospitals (50 beds and under, ~1/3 of US hospital market). Came with ancillary lab, pharmacy, and radiology modules.\n- **webOMR from Beth Israel Deaconess Medical Center** (acquired February 2015) -- One of the first hospital-built inpatient/outpatient EHR systems, developed over 30 years. Intended to accelerate entry into 100+ bed hospitals (~48% of US hospitals).\n- **University of Toledo Medical Center co-development** (announced 2016) -- Partnership to build the inpatient product into a \"disruptive alternative to legacy health IT.\"\n\nThe result is **athenaOne for Hospitals & Health Systems**, which integrates these acquired technologies with athenahealth's ambulatory platform. It is certified for:\n- Computerized Provider Order Entry (CPOE) for medications, laboratory, diagnostic imaging\n- Drug-drug / drug-allergy interaction checks\n- Inpatient medication orders\n\nIt targets critical access hospitals, small hospitals, and health systems needing integrated inpatient + ambulatory EHR on a cloud platform. The v25 certification was achieved March 21, 2025.\n\nSources:\n- [athenahealth Hospital IT Strategy - HCI Innovation Group](https://www.hcinnovationgroup.com/finance-revenue-cycle/article/13026930/most-interesting-vendors-2016-athenahealth-accelerating-its-growth-in-hospital-it)\n- [athenahealth-Beth Israel EHR Deal - (Fifth award category not specified in sources but confirmed as five total)\n\n**Key KLAS findings:**\n- KLAS positions athenahealth alongside Epic as \"clear market leaders\" in ambulatory care, with customers reporting high satisfaction with the \"cohesive package\" for both clinical and operational needs.\n- KLAS notes ambulatory practices are \"ditching point solutions\" in favor of integrated suites from Epic and athenahealth.\n- The awards are based on thousands of interviews with clinicians and practice administrators.\n\n---\n\n## Source 3: Capterra / Software Advice / GetApp Reviews\n\n**URLs visited:**\n- [Capterra athenaOne reviews](https://www.capterra.com/p/98735/athenaOne/reviews/)\n- [Software Advice athenaOne profile](https://www.softwareadvice.com/medical/athenacollector-athenaclinicals-profile/)\n- [Software Advice athenaOne reviews](https://www.softwareadvice.com/medical/athenacollector-athenaclinicals-profile/reviews/)\n\n**Ratings:** 3.8/5 stars based on ~835-906 reviews across Capterra/Software Advice (these share a review pool).\n\n**What users say about specific features:**\n\n- **Charting / Clinical Documentation:** Users report charting takes \"half the time compared to Epic.\" Patient chart layouts are customizable, with text boxes under each tab functioning as individual widgets. Users can access last visits, lab orders, and medications in one view. Predefined text boxes and customizable workflows are praised. However, multiple clicks are sometimes needed for simple tasks, and template creation can be confusing.\n\n- **Scheduling:** Appointment scheduling is described as \"easy and intuitive\" with clear patient selection and confirmation. The calendar supports daily, weekly, and monthly views, and shows how many patients are in the office. Complaints: the calendar function can be \"clunky for new users,\" with occasional glitches like duplicated provider names and inability to block schedules directly from the calendar.\n\n- **E-Prescribing:** Users can review active medications and history, modify medications, check refills, order new prescriptions, and check drug-drug and drug-allergy interactions. PDMP (Prescription Drug Monitoring Program) integration is available. This is generally well-regarded.\n\n- **Billing / Revenue Cycle:** The billing engine has 30,000+ rules for catching errors before submission, with 4,500+ rule changes per year to stay current with payer policies. Claims achieve a 94% first-pass resolution rate. athenaIDX achieves a 91% clean claim rate within 3 months. However, billing is also a top complaint area: users report aggressive patient statements, problems with Medicaid billing, claims put on hold without follow-up, and one practice reported no claims sent for 6+ weeks after go-live due to configuration issues.\n\n- **Patient Portal / Patient Engagement:** Patients can request appointments, refill prescriptions, send secure messages, update demographics, update pharmacy/medication/allergy information, and pay bills. Over 90% of telehealth patients left 4- or 5-star reviews. The athenaPatient mobile app is available on iOS and Android.\n\n- **Reporting:** Described as an area where athenahealth \"particularly shines.\" Features include on-demand access to a data store, browser-based SQL editor optimized for analytics and bulk extraction, and the ability to connect the backend data store to a BI tool or data warehouse. Users can run reports and create custom reports.\n\n**Top complaints (recurring themes):**\n1. **Customer support** is the single most cited complaint. Slow response times (10-30 minutes per question, escalations take 7-10 days), inexperienced reps, email/chat-only contact with no easy phone option.\n2. **Implementation difficulties:** One practice reported an extra 4 months and $60,000 beyond plan. Another described going live as \"one of the most disruptive, financially damaging, and exhausting experiences of their career.\"\n3. **System performance:** Freezing, slow load times, frequent bugs and crashes.\n4. **Billing gaps:** Despite marketing as end-to-end RCM, users say collections efforts are minimal (\"stopped after sending just two emails to patients\"), requiring supplemental staff.\n5. **Missing MFA by default** was noted as a security concern.\n6. **Community moderation:** Users report athenahealth \"openly bans anyone who makes repeated negative comments\" in its community support area.\n\n---\n\n## Source 4: Market Data (Definitive Healthcare, 6sense, Enlyft)\n\n**URLs visited:**\n- [Definitive Healthcare top ambulatory EHR Clinical charting and documentation (customizable templates, voice-to-text)\n2. Appointment scheduling (calendar views, patient status tracking)\n3. It combines three core modules:\n\n1. **athenaClinicals** -- Electronic Health Record (EHR/EMR)\n2. **athenaCollector** -- Revenue Cycle Management / Practice Management / Billing\n3. **athenaCommunicator** -- Patient Engagement\n\nTogether, these three capabilities constitute athenaOne. The platform runs on \"athenaNet,\" the underlying network and technology infrastructure.\n\n#### athenaClinicals (The EHR Module)\n\nathenaClinicals is specifically the electronic health records / electronic medical records component within athenaOne. It handles:\n- Electronic medical charting and clinical documentation (SOAP notes, encounter documentation)\n- Prescription management and e-prescribing\n- Lab and diagnostic test ordering\n- Digital health records (patient history, medical records, lab results, medication histories)\n- Clinical task management\n- Telehealth / virtual visits\n- Quality program support (MIPS, MACRA, CQMs)\n- Clinical decision support (CDS) with real-time alerts for drug interactions, allergies, patient-specific information\n- Embedded Epocrates for drug reference and clinical decision support\n- Population health and care gap tracking\n- Same-day encounter close rate tracking\n\nFrom the CHPL metadata in the repository, athenaClinicals (ambulatory) v25 is ONC-certified (CHPL ID 11714, certified 2025-10-28) and includes certifications for:\n- (a)(1)-(a)(5): CPOE, demographics, problem list, medication list, medication allergy list\n- (a)(12): Family health history\n- (a)(14)-(a)(15): Implantable device list, social/psychological/behavioral data\n- (b)(1)-(b)(3): Transitions of care, clinical information reconciliation, electronic prescribing\n- (b)(9)-(b)(11): Care plan, bulk data export, EHI export\n- (c)(1)-(c)(3): Clinical quality measures (CQMs) -- record, export, report (ambulatory version only)\n- (d)(1)-(d)(13): Security, authentication, audit, encryption, integrity\n- (e)(1),(e)(3): View/download/transmit, patient health information\n- (f)(1),(f)(2),(f)(5),(f)(7): Immunization registry, cancer registry, syndromic surveillance\n- (g)(2)-(g)(10): Automated testing, safety-enhanced design, accessibility, FHIR API\n- (h)(1): Direct messaging\n\n#### athenaClinicals for Hospitals and Health Systems\n\nThis is a **separate ONC-certified product** (CHPL ID 11613, certified 2025-03-21), distinct from the ambulatory version. Key differences:\n- Designed for the additional requirements of a **non-ambulatory / inpatient / hospital setting**\n- Includes inpatient nursing documentation, patient floor management, Medical Surgical department workflows\n- Supports discharge planning with automatic generation of discharge instructions and summaries\n- Enables flow of discharge summaries and hospital encounters directly into ambulatory workflows\n- Does NOT include (c)(1)-(c)(3) CQM criteria (the ambulatory version does)\n- Otherwise shares largely the same certification criteria as the ambulatory version\n\nBoth products are offered as part of comprehensive solutions: athenaOne (ambulatory) and athenaOne for Hospitals and Health Systems (inpatient), respectively.\n\n#### athenaCollector (Billing / Practice Management / RCM)\n\nathenaCollector is the billing and practice management module within athenaOne. It handles:\n- Claims submission (over 315 million claims per year across the network)\n- Claims scrubbing with 30,000+ rules before electronic submission\n- Revenue cycle management\n- Coding and charge entry\n- Eligibility checking (within 5 days prior to service date)\n- Payment processing and posting\n- Patient billing and collections\n- Days in Accounts Receivable (DAR) tracking\n- Time-of-service copay collections\n- Denial management and rework\n- Electronic remittance advice (ERA) processing\n- Provider enrollment management\n\n#### athenaCommunicator (Patient Engagement)\n\nathenaCommunicator is the patient engagement module. It includes:\n- Secure messaging between patients and providers\n- Appointment reminders (\"ReminderCall\")\n- Self-scheduling for patients\n- Live operator access\n- Patient portal (athenaPatient app)\n- Digital intake forms (medical history, consent, demographics) that pre-populate charts\n- Population health campaigns (wellness visits, vaccination reminders, cancer screening, chronic disease management)\n- Flexible payment options\n\n#### athenaIDX (Enterprise RCM -- Separate Product)\n\nathenaIDX is a **separate** enterprise revenue cycle management product, distinct from athenaCollector. Key facts:\n- **Formerly known as Centricity Business** (rebranded to athenaIDX in July 2020)\n- Originally developed by IDX Systems Corporation (Burlington, VT), acquired by GE Healthcare in 2006\n- Came to athenahealth through the Virence Health acquisition\n- Optimized for **large practices, health systems, billing services, and hospitals**\n- Available as on-premise or cloud-based deployment (unlike athenaOne which is cloud-only)\n- Provides a consolidated view of all hospital and professional financial data across an enterprise\n- Single RCM solution that consolidates hospital and physician group scheduling, billing, and balances\n- Linked to on the athenahealth.com footer as a separate solution: https://www.athenahealth.com/solutions/revenue-cycle-management\n\n#### acquisition\n- Separately ONC-certified from athenaClinicals\n- Has its own Real World Testing plans\n- Listed with athenaFlow for ONC cost disclosures\n\n#### athenaFlow (Legacy Product)\n\nathenaFlow is another **legacy product** from the GE Centricity family:\n- Also came through the Virence Health acquisition\n- Separately ONC-certified\n- Listed together with athenaPractice for ONC cost disclosures\n\n#### Epocrates\n\nEpocrates is a clinical decision support and drug reference mobile app:\n- Acquired by athenahealth in 2013\n- Aggregates treatment information including dosing and contraindications\n- Provides clinical decision support at the point of prescribing\n- Integrated into athenaClinicals\n- Has its own set of patents\n\n#### Additional It combines three core modules:\n\n1. **athenaClinicals** -- Electronic Health Record (EHR/EMR)\n2. **athenaCollector** -- Revenue Cycle Management / Practice Management / Billing\n3. **athenaCommunicator** -- Patient Engagement\n\nTogether, these three capabilities constitute athenaOne. The platform runs on \"athenaNet,\" the underlying network and technology infrastructure.\n\n#### athenaClinicals (The EHR Module)\n\nathenaClinicals is specifically the electronic health records / electronic medical records component within athenaOne. It handles:\n- Electronic medical charting and clinical documentation (SOAP notes, encounter documentation)\n- Prescription management and e-prescribing\n- Lab and diagnostic test ordering\n- Digital health records (patient history, medical records, lab results, medication histories)\n- Clinical task management\n- Telehealth / virtual visits\n- Quality program support (MIPS, MACRA, CQMs)\n- Clinical decision support (CDS) with real-time alerts for drug interactions, allergies, patient-specific information\n- Embedded Epocrates for drug reference and clinical decision support\n- Population health and care gap tracking\n- Same-day encounter close rate tracking\n\nFrom the CHPL metadata in the repository, athenaClinicals (ambulatory) v25 is ONC-certified (CHPL ID 11714, certified 2025-10-28) and includes certifications for:\n- (a)(1)-(a)(5): CPOE, demographics, problem list, medication list, medication allergy list\n- (a)(12): Family health history\n- (a)(14)-(a)(15): Implantable device list, social/psychological/behavioral data\n- (b)(1)-(b)(3): Transitions of care, clinical information reconciliation, electronic prescribing\n- (b)(9)-(b)(11): Care plan, bulk data export, EHI export\n- (c)(1)-(c)(3): Clinical quality measures (CQMs) -- record, export, report (ambulatory version only)\n- (d)(1)-(d)(13): Security, authentication, audit, encryption, integrity\n- (e)(1),(e)(3): View/download/transmit, patient health information\n- (f)(1),(f)(2),(f)(5),(f)(7): Immunization registry, cancer registry, syndromic surveillance\n- (g)(2)-(g)(10): Automated testing, safety-enhanced design, accessibility, FHIR API\n- (h)(1): Direct messaging\n\n#### athenaClinicals for Hospitals and Health Systems\n\nThis is a **separate ONC-certified product** (CHPL ID 11613, certified 2025-03-21), distinct from the ambulatory version. Key differences:\n- Designed for the additional requirements of a **non-ambulatory / inpatient / hospital setting**\n- Includes inpatient nursing documentation, patient floor management, Medical Surgical department workflows\n- Supports discharge planning with automatic generation of discharge instructions and summaries\n- Enables flow of discharge summaries and hospital encounters directly into ambulatory workflows\n- Does NOT include (c)(1)-(c)(3) CQM criteria (the ambulatory version does)\n- Otherwise shares largely the same certification criteria as the ambulatory version\n\nBoth products are offered as part of comprehensive solutions: athenaOne (ambulatory) and athenaOne for Hospitals and Health Systems (inpatient), respectively.\n\n#### athenaCollector (Billing / Practice Management / RCM)\n\nathenaCollector is the billing and practice management module within athenaOne. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_019LAivsxD4bBYG3f2xnthBK","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01E9LqHwSyVe8tyER1uwdLqH","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/jmandel/hobby/ehi-export-analysis/results/athenahealth-inc--athenaclinicals/product-research.md","content":"# athenahealth, Inc. — Product Research\n\nResearched: 2026-02-16\nDeveloper website: https://www.athenahealth.com\n\n## Overview\n\nathenahealth, Inc. is one of the largest ambulatory EHR vendors in the United States, serving 170,000+ providers across approximately 6,700–6,900 customer organizations. The company was founded in 1997 by Jonathan Bush and Todd Park, went public on NASDAQ in 2007, was taken private in 2018 by Veritas Capital and Evergreen Coast Capital (Elliott Management subsidiary) for $5.7B, and was subsequently acquired by Bain Capital and Hellman & Friedman in 2022 for $17B. It is currently privately held, headquartered in Boston, Massachusetts, with approximately 6,300 employees.\n\nathenahealth's flagship platform is **athenaOne**, a fully integrated, cloud-based SaaS platform combining EHR, practice management/billing, and patient engagement. The company's go-to-market is primarily ambulatory/outpatient practices, ranging from solo practitioners to large multi-site health systems and FQHCs, though they also serve hospitals (particularly critical access and community hospitals under ~100 beds). They support 60+ medical specialties. Per KLAS Research, athenahealth has won 47 lifetime Best in KLAS awards and is positioned alongside Epic as a \"clear market leader\" in ambulatory care, with particular strength in the 11–75 physician independent practice segment.\n\nKey corporate history: In 2013, athenahealth acquired Epocrates (clinical decision support / drug reference app) for $293M. In 2015, they acquired RazorInsights (cloud EHR for small hospitals) and webOMR from Beth Israel Deaconess Medical Center (inpatient EHR). In 2019, after the Veritas acquisition, athenahealth merged with Virence Health (formerly GE Healthcare's Clinical Business Solutions division), which brought in the Centricity product line — later rebranded as athenaPractice, athenaFlow, and athenaIDX. These legacy Centricity products remain separately ONC-certified.\n\n## Product: athenaClinicals (Ambulatory)\n\nCHPL ID: 11714\n\n### What It Is\n\nathenaClinicals is the EHR/EMR module within the athenaOne platform. It is the clinical documentation and clinical workflow component — but it does not stand alone; it is tightly integrated with athenaCollector (billing/practice management) and athenaCommunicator (patient engagement) as parts of the unified athenaOne platform. The certified module is athenaClinicals, but the product that practices actually purchase and use is athenaOne, which includes all three modules plus ancillary capabilities like telehealth, Epocrates, the Marketplace, and analytics.\n\nathenaOne is fully cloud-based SaaS, running on athenahealth's \"athenaNet\" infrastructure. There is no on-premise deployment option for athenaOne. The platform is browser-based with mobile apps for patients (athenaPatient on iOS/Android).\n\n### Users & Market\n\n**Who uses it:** Ambulatory/outpatient clinicians and support staff — physicians, nurses, medical assistants, billing staff, practice managers, and patients (via the portal). Supports 60+ specialties including primary care, cardiology, dermatology, pediatrics, orthopedics, OB-GYN, behavioral health, gastroenterology, and many others. Specialty-specific workflow packages (e.g., athenaOne for Women's Health, athenaOne for Urgent Care) launched in 2025.\n\n**Scale:** 170,000+ providers on the network; serves over 20% of the U.S. population; submits 315M+ claims per year. Approximately 6,700–6,900 customer organizations. Per Enlyft, 64% of customers are small organizations (<50 employees), 24% medium, 12% large (>1,000 employees) — but 13% have revenue over $1B, indicating a mix of small practices and large health systems. 95% of customers are in the United States.\n\n**Market position:** Third-largest ambulatory EHR vendor by installation count (5,801 per Definitive Healthcare, October 2025), behind Epic (16,295) and eClinicalWorks (9,966). Rated #1 by KLAS for Independent Physician Practice Suite (3 consecutive years), Ambulatory EHR 11–75 physicians (5 consecutive years), and Practice Management 11–75 physicians (4 consecutive years). In 2026, won first-time recognition for Ambulatory EHR 75+ physicians, signaling enterprise expansion. G2 rating: 3.5/5; Capterra: 3.8/5 across 835+ reviews.\n\n**Notable user feedback (from Capterra/G2/KLAS):** Users praise integrated scheduling/charting/billing workflow, customizable chart templates, reporting capabilities, and first-pass claim resolution rates. Top complaints are customer support responsiveness, implementation difficulties (cost overruns, extended timelines), system performance/freezing, and gaps between sales promises and delivered functionality. Mobile capabilities and telehealth rated below competitors.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nThe athenaOne platform consists of three core modules plus ancillary capabilities:\n\n**athenaClinicals (EHR):**\n- Clinical documentation — SOAP notes, encounter documentation, specialty-specific templates, voice recognition/dictation\n- Computerized Provider Order Entry (CPOE) for medications, labs, and diagnostic imaging\n- Electronic prescribing (e-prescribing) including EPCS for controlled substances, with PDMP integration and drug-drug/drug-allergy interaction checking\n- Lab and imaging order management with results review\n- Problem list, medication list, medication allergy list management\n- Patient history (past medical, surgical, family, OB history)\n- Vital signs and clinical observations\n- Immunization tracking\n- Implantable device tracking\n- Social, psychological, and behavioral data capture\n- Care plans and care team management\n- Clinical decision support (via embedded Epocrates — drug reference, interaction checking)\n- Quality measure support (MIPS, MACRA, CQMs) — note: CQM certification (c)(1)–(c)(3) is ambulatory-only\n- Document management (scanned, faxed, digital documents)\n- Referral management (via athenaCoordinator module)\n- Population health / care gap tracking\n- AI features (2025): ambient-sourced diagnosis suggestions, clinically inferred diagnoses, Chart Assist (GenAI for chart review and summarization), AI document labeling\n\n**athenaCollector (Billing / Practice Management / RCM):**\n- Appointment scheduling (daily/weekly/monthly calendar views, patient status tracking, self-scheduling)\n- Patient registration and demographics\n- Insurance eligibility verification\n- Claims submission with 30,000+ automated scrubbing rules (94% first-pass resolution rate)\n- Charge entry and medical coding (with AI-assisted coding in 2025)\n- Electronic remittance advice (ERA) processing\n- Payment posting\n- Denial management with root-cause analysis\n- Patient billing and collections / statements\n- Authorization management\n- Provider enrollment\n- Days in Accounts Receivable (DAR) tracking\n- Copay collection at time of service\n- Reporting and analytics\n- **Business model note:** athenahealth charges a percentage of client collections rather than licensing fees\n\n**athenaCommunicator (Patient Engagement):**\n- Patient portal (web-based + athenaPatient mobile app on iOS/Android)\n- Secure HIPAA-compliant messaging between patients and care team\n- Appointment reminders (\"ReminderCall\") — reduces no-show rate by 8%\n- Self-scheduling\n- Digital intake forms (medical history, consent, demographics that pre-populate charts)\n- Online check-in\n- Bill pay / payment management\n- Population health outreach campaigns (wellness visits, vaccination reminders, cancer screening, chronic disease management)\n- Mass messaging capabilities\n- Lab result notifications\n- Care plan access for patients\n\n**athenaTelehealth:**\n- HIPAA-compliant video visits from any browser/device with camera\n- Up to 4 participants per session\n- Live closed captions and transcripts\n- Two-way messaging during visits\n- Fully integrated with scheduling and billing workflow\n- 3,000+ active customers\n- Patients can join from athenaPatient app or web portal\n\n**Epocrates (Clinical Decision Support):**\n- Drug reference and dosing information\n- Drug-drug and drug-allergy interaction checking\n- Clinical decision support at the point of prescribing\n- Acquired by athenahealth in 2013; integrated into athenaClinicals\n\n**athenaCoordinator (Care Coordination):**\n- Referral management\n- Order transmission between providers\n- Referral-related denial tracking\n\n**Marketplace (Third-Party Integrations):**\n- 500+ vetted third-party applications across 50 digital health capabilities and 60 specialties\n- Direct integration with athenaOne via APIs\n- Categories include: clinical workflow, patient engagement, billing optimization, AI tools, specialty-specific solutions\n\n**Interoperability / Data Exchange:**\n- CommonWell Health Alliance (90% of customers connected; 84,000+ care sites)\n- Carequality participation\n- TEFCA connectivity\n- Direct messaging (h)(1)\n- FHIR R4 APIs with SMART on FHIR support\n- 800+ REST API endpoints\n- HL7 v2 interfaces\n- C-CDA document exchange\n- Bulk FHIR export\n- EHI export\n- Public health reporting: immunization registries, syndromic surveillance, cancer registries\n\n**Reporting & Analytics:**\n- On-demand SQL access to backend data store via browser-based editor\n- BI tool connectivity (connect backend data store to external data warehouses)\n- Custom report creation\n- Insights dashboards\n- Quality measure reporting\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on vendor documentation, API specifications, FHIR resource listings from the EHI export documentation, and user reviews, athenaOne stores and manages the following categories of data:\n\n**Clinical Data:** Patient demographics, medical/surgical/family/OB history, problem lists (conditions/diagnoses), medication lists (active/completed/historical), medication allergy lists, allergies and intolerances, encounters/visits, clinical notes and documentation (SOAP notes, encounter summaries), lab orders and results, imaging orders and results, vital signs and clinical observations, immunization records, procedures, care plans, care team assignments, clinical impressions/assessments, goals, family member history, consent records, implantable device information, social/psychological/behavioral data, referral records, prescriptions/e-prescribing records (including controlled substance prescriptions), clinical decision support alerts, scanned/faxed/digital documents, binary attachments, clinical images.\n\n**Billing/Financial Data:** Insurance coverage and eligibility records, claims (submitted/tracked/adjudicated), charges, payments, adjustments, billing statements, collections records, deductibles, patient insurance information, accounts, coding data, ERA/remittance data, denial tracking, authorization records, provider enrollment data. (Sources: athenaCollector features page; FHIR EHI export resource list includes custom resources: Adjustment, BillingStatement, Charge, Claim, Collection, Deductible, Eligibility, PatientInsurance, Payment.)\n\n**Scheduling/Administrative Data:** Appointments, schedules, time slots, patient check-in/check-out records, no-show tracking, provider/practitioner information, organization/location data, account information.\n\n**Patient Engagement Data:** Secure messages between patients and providers, appointment reminder logs, patient portal activity, digital intake form submissions (demographics, medical history, consent), population health campaign/outreach records, telehealth visit records and transcripts.\n\n**Audit/System Data:** Provenance records, audit logs.\n\nThe EHI export documentation references FHIR R4 resources in three categories: system resources (Location, Medication, Organization, Practitioner, PractitionerRole), clinical resources (AllergyIntolerance, Binary, CarePlan, CareTeam, ClinicalImpression, Condition, Consent, Device, DiagnosticReport, DocumentReference, Encounter, FamilyMemberHistory, Goal, Immunization, MedicationAdministration, MedicationRequest, MedicationStatement, Observation, Procedure, Provenance, ServiceRequest), and practice management resources (Account, Appointment, Coverage, Patient, RelatedPerson, Schedule, Slot) — plus custom billing resources.\n\n---\n\n## Product: athenaClinicals for Hospitals and Health Systems\n\nCHPL ID: 11613\n\n### What It Is\n\nathenaClinicals for Hospitals and Health Systems is a **separate ONC-certified product** from the ambulatory athenaClinicals, designed for inpatient/hospital settings. It is part of **athenaOne for Hospitals & Health Systems**, which integrates hospital EHR capabilities with the ambulatory athenaOne platform.\n\nCritically, the hospital product was **assembled from multiple acquisitions** and is **not the same codebase** as the ambulatory product:\n- **RazorInsights** (acquired January 2015) — cloud-based EHR for rural, critical access, and community hospitals (50 beds and under). Came with ancillary lab, pharmacy, and radiology modules.\n- **webOMR from Beth Israel Deaconess Medical Center** (acquired February 2015) — one of the first hospital-built inpatient/outpatient EHR systems, developed over 30 years. Intended to accelerate entry into 100+ bed hospitals.\n- **University of Toledo Medical Center co-development** (announced 2016) — partnership to build the inpatient product into a \"disruptive alternative to legacy health IT.\"\n\nThe certification criteria are nearly identical to the ambulatory version, with one key difference: the hospital version does **not** include (c)(1)–(c)(3) clinical quality measure criteria. Both versions share the same v25 version number. The hospital version was certified March 21, 2025.\n\n### Users & Market\n\n**Target users:** Inpatient clinicians and support staff — physicians, nurses, hospital administrators, discharge planners, and ancillary staff (lab, pharmacy, radiology).\n\n**Target settings:** Critical access hospitals, community hospitals, small hospitals (originally ~50 beds and under via the RazorInsights heritage, expanding to 100+ beds via the webOMR acquisition). The product integrates with the ambulatory athenaOne platform to provide a unified inpatient + outpatient experience for health systems.\n\n**Market position:** athenahealth is primarily known as an ambulatory vendor. The hospital product is a newer, smaller part of their portfolio. The 2015 acquisitions and 2016 co-development partnership were described as athenahealth's strategy to \"accelerate growth in hospital IT\" and create a \"disruptive alternative to legacy health IT.\" The product targets hospitals that find Epic or Oracle Health (Cerner) too expensive or complex.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nThe hospital product adds inpatient-specific capabilities on top of the athenaOne platform:\n\n- Inpatient clinical documentation / nursing documentation\n- Hospital CPOE for medications, laboratory, and diagnostic imaging\n- Drug-drug and drug-allergy interaction checks in the inpatient context\n- Inpatient medication orders\n- Patient floor management\n- Discharge planning with automatic generation of discharge instructions and summaries\n- Flow of discharge summaries and hospital encounters directly into ambulatory workflows\n- Ancillary modules: lab, pharmacy, radiology (from RazorInsights heritage)\n- Medical-Surgical department workflows\n- Integration with ambulatory athenaClinicals for continuity across settings\n\nThe hospital product is offered as part of a comprehensive suite alongside the ambulatory billing, scheduling, and patient engagement modules (athenaCollector, athenaCommunicator).\n\n### Data & Content\n\nThe hospital product stores the same categories of clinical data as the ambulatory version (demographics, conditions, medications, allergies, encounters, orders, results, notes, documents, etc.) plus hospital-specific data:\n\n- Inpatient encounter records and nursing documentation\n- Inpatient medication administration records\n- Discharge summaries and discharge instructions\n- Hospital floor/bed management data\n- Ancillary department data (lab, pharmacy, radiology)\n- Hospital-specific orders and results\n\nThe certification criteria overlap significantly with ambulatory, including transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(3), patient portal access (e)(1), public health reporting (f)(1),(f)(2),(f)(5),(f)(7), FHIR APIs (g)(10), and EHI export (b)(10)–(b)(11). The EHI export documentation (https://docs.athenahealth.com/athenaone-dataexports/) appears to cover both products.\n\n---\n\n## Other Products in the athenahealth Ecosystem\n\n**athenaIDX** — A separate enterprise-scale revenue cycle management product, distinct from athenaCollector. Originally IDX Systems Corporation → GE Centricity Business → rebranded athenaIDX in July 2020. Targets large practices, health systems, hospitals, and billing services. Available on-premise or cloud (unlike athenaOne which is cloud-only). Separately certified and marketed.\n\n**athenaPractice** — A legacy EHR product, formerly GE Centricity EMR/Practice Solution. Came through the Virence Health/GE Healthcare acquisition. Separately ONC-certified with its own Real World Testing plans. Legacy product being maintained alongside athenaOne.\n\n**athenaFlow** — Another legacy product from the GE Centricity family. Separately ONC-certified. Listed together with athenaPractice for ONC cost disclosures.\n\nThese products are relevant context because they share the athenahealth brand and infrastructure but are distinct systems with different codebases, certifications, and customer bases. The EHI export obligations for athenaClinicals and athenaClinicals for Hospitals should cover data stored by the athenaOne platform (including athenaCollector and athenaCommunicator data), not just the clinical module in isolation.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":8941,"cache_creation_input_tokens":7713,"cache_read_input_tokens":29444,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":7713},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dfabe0df-7a0b-4f07-a4fd-79f9f540796c","uuid":"41df6b23-906e-4689-a160-697c287627ed"}
